For
\lambda
with core
\kappa(\lambda)
compatible with
N_{\bullet}
according to (
2.78
) and
\ell(\lambda)\leq|N_{\bullet}|
, the wreath Macdonald polynomial
P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]
satisfies the equations:

\displaystyle D_{p,n}^{*}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]

\displaystyle=e_{n}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+1\end{subarray}}^{|N_{\bullet}|}q^{-\lambda_{b}}t^{-|N_{\bullet}|+b}\right]P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]

\displaystyle D_{p,n}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]

\displaystyle=e_{n}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+1\end{subarray}}^{|N_{\bullet}|}q^{\lambda_{b}}t^{|N_{\bullet}|-b}\right]P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t].

Here,
x_{i,l}
,
q
, and
t
take generic values.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})
and
\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}^{*}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})
be the eigenvalues of
D_{p,n}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1})
and
D_{p,n}^{*}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1})
, respectively, at
P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1}]
.
Combining (
5.47
), (
5.48
), and Lemma
5.19
, we have

\displaystyle t^{-n|N_{\bullet}|}\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}^{*}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}c_{p,k,n}^{+}\mathfrak{e}_{p,k}^{*}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})

(5.53)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{t^{-n|N_{\bullet}|-(n-k)(p+1)-r\binom{n-k}{2}}}{\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{n-k}(1-t^{-rl})}e_{k}\left[\sum_{a=1}^{N_{p}}t^{|N_{\bullet}|}\left(s^{(p)}_{a}\right)^{-1}\right]

and

\displaystyle t^{n|N_{\bullet}|}\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}c_{p,k,n}^{-}\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})

(5.54)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{t^{n|N_{\bullet}|+(n-k)(p+1)+r\binom{n-k}{2}}}{\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{n-k}(1-t^{rl})}e_{k}\left[\sum_{a=1}^{N_{p}}t^{-|N_{\bullet}|}s^{(p)}_{a}\right]

We can induct on
n
to show that, as functions of
\lambda
,
\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})
is polynomial in
\{s_{\bullet}^{(p)}\}
and
\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}^{*}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})
is polynomial in
\{(s^{(p)}_{\bullet})^{-1}\}
.
Applying (
5.52
)
n
times, we have (when viewed as operators):

\displaystyle D_{p,n}^{*}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1})\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{l=1}^{N_{i}}x^{(i)}_{l}=q^{-nr}\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{l=1}^{N_{i}}x^{(i)}_{l}D_{p,n}^{*}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1})

\displaystyle D_{p,n}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1})\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{l=1}^{N_{i}}x^{(i)}_{l}=q^{nr}\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{l=1}^{N_{i}}x^{(i)}_{l}D_{p,n}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1}).

It then follows from Proposition
5.20
that
\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})
is homogeneous of degree
n
and
\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}^{*}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})
is homogeneous of degree
-n
.
Thus,
t^{-n|N_{\bullet}|}\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}^{*}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})
is the degree
-n
piece of (
5.53
) and
t^{n|N_{\bullet}|}\mathfrak{e}_{p,n}(\lambda;q,t^{-1})
is the degree
n
piece of (
5.54
).
This establishes the eigenvalue equations under the appropriate conditions (
5.8
) and (
5.9
) on
x^{(i)}_{l}
,
q
, and
t
.
We extend to generic values as in the proof of Theorem
5.11
.
∎

Remark 5.22
.

Even though
r\geq 3
was assumed throughout, we have verified experimentally that Theorem
5.21
continues to hold as stated for
r=2
.
The
r=1
case is discussed in Remark
5.15
above.

Example 5.23
.

Let
r=2
,
p=1
,
N_{\bullet}=(0,2)
, and
\lambda=(1)
.
Because
\lambda
is a
2
-core,

P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]=1

There are only two shift patterns containing
1
:

\displaystyle{\underline{J}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{x_{1}^{(1)}\}

\displaystyle{\underline{J}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\{x_{2}^{(1)}\}.

Note that

\displaystyle T_{{\underline{J}}_{1}}x_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=q^{2}x_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle T_{{\underline{J}}_{2}}x_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=x_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle T_{{\underline{J}}_{1}}x_{2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=x_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle T_{{\underline{J}}_{2}}x_{2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=q^{2}x_{2}^{(1)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle D_{1,1}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t)P_{\lambda}[X_{\bullet};q,t]

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)(1-qt^{-1})}{1-q^{2}t^{-2}}\left\{\frac{qtx_{2}^{(1)}-q^{2}x_{1}^{(1)}}{x_{1}^{(1)}-x_{2}^{(1)}}+\frac{qtx_{1}^{(1)}-q^{2}x_{2}^{(1)}}{x_{2}^{(1)}-x_{1}^{(1)}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)(1-qt^{-1})(-qt-q^{2})}{1-q^{2}t^{-2}}

\displaystyle=qtP_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t].

Appendix A
Wreath Noumi-Sano operators

In this appendix, we apply our methods to study wreath analogues of the trigonometric
Noumi-Sano operators

[
12
]
.
We obtain explicit formulas for degree
n=1
and an integral formula for general
n
.

A.1.
Infinite-variable eigenvalues

Let
(x;y)_{\infty}
denote the infinite
y
-Pochammer symbol:

(x;y)_{\infty}=\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}(1-xy^{i}).

Lemma A.1
.

Assume
|q^{\pm 1}|<1
and
|t^{\pm 1}|<1
(where ‘
+
’ and ‘
-
’ are separate cases).
For
p\in I
, we have

(A.1)

\displaystyle\left\langle\lambda\left|\exp\left[-\sum_{k>0}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{r}q^{\pm k(i-1)}h_{p+i,\pm k}}{(1-q^{\pm kr})}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\pm k}z^{\mp k}}{v^{\pm k}[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\right]\right|\lambda\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\exp\left[\sum_{k>0}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac{q^{\pm k(i-1)}}{1-q^{\pm kr}}\left\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b>0\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+i\end{subarray}}q^{\pm k\lambda_{b}}t^{\pm kb}-t^{\mp k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b>0\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+i+1\end{subarray}}q^{\pm k\lambda_{b}}t^{\pm kb}\right\}\right)\frac{z^{\mp k}}{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b>0\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+i+1\end{subarray}}\left(q^{\pm(\lambda_{b}+i)}t^{\pm(b-1)}z^{\mp 1};q^{\pm r}\right)_{\infty}}{\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b>0\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+i\end{subarray}}\left(q^{\pm(\lambda_{b}+i)}t^{\pm b}z^{\mp 1};q^{\pm r}\right)_{\infty}}

where we set
\lambda_{b}=0
for all
b>\ell(\lambda)
.

A.2.
Shuffle elements

We rewrite

\displaystyle\varsigma\exp\left[-\sum_{k>0}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{r}q^{\pm k(i-1)}h_{p+i,\pm k}}{(1-q^{\pm kr})}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\pm k}z^{\mp k}}{[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\varsigma\exp\left[\sum_{k>0}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}q^{\mp k(i+1)}h_{p-i,\pm k}}{(1-q^{\mp kr})}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\pm k}z^{\mp k}}{[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\eta\exp\left[-\sum_{k>0}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}t^{\mp k(i+1)}\varsigma^{-1}(h_{p-i,\mp k})}{(1-t^{\mp kr})}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\pm k}z^{\mp k}}{[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\eta\exp\left[(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})^{-1}\sum_{k>0}\left(-q^{\pm k}\varsigma^{-1}(h_{p,\mp k}^{\perp})+\varsigma^{-1}(h_{p+1,\mp k}^{\perp})\right)\frac{z^{\mp k}}{k}\right].

Recall the formulas (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) for
\mathcal{E}_{p,n}^{\pm}
and
\mathcal{H}_{p,n}^{\pm}
.
In
[
19
]
, it was shown that

\displaystyle\exp\left[(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})^{-1}\sum_{k>0}\left(-q^{-k}\varsigma^{-1}(h_{p,k}^{\perp})+\varsigma^{-1}(h_{p+1,k}^{\perp})\right)\frac{z^{k}}{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{nr}t^{nr}\mathfrak{d}^{-n}\left(1-q^{-1}t^{-1}\right)^{nr}}{\mathfrak{q}^{2n}\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{-r}t^{-r}\right)}\Psi_{+}\left(\mathcal{E}_{p,n}^{-}\right)

\displaystyle\exp\left[(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})^{-1}\sum_{k>0}\left(-q^{k}\varsigma^{-1}(h_{p,-k}^{\perp})+\varsigma^{-1}(h_{p+1,-k}^{\perp})\right)\frac{z^{-k}}{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{n}\left(1-qt\right)^{nr}}{\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{-r}t^{-r}\right)}\Psi_{-}\left(\mathcal{E}_{p,n}^{+}\right).

Applying
\eta
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{n(r-1)}t^{-n}\left(1-q^{-1}t^{-1}\right)^{nr}}{v^{-n}\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{-r}t^{-r}\right)}\Psi_{+}(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{p,n})

\displaystyle=\varsigma\exp\left[-\sum_{k>0}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}q^{-ki}h_{p+i,-k}}{(1-q^{-kr})}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{-k}z^{k}}{v^{-k}[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\right],

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{nr}\mathfrak{d}^{-n(r-1)}t^{n}\left(1-qt\right)^{nr}}{v^{n}q^{n}\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{-r}t^{-r}\right)}\Psi_{-}\left(\mathcal{H}_{p,n}^{+}\right)

\displaystyle=\varsigma\exp\left[-\sum_{k>0}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}q^{ki}h_{p+i,k}}{(1-q^{kr})}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{k}z^{-k}}{v^{k}[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\right].

A.3.
Normal ordering

It will be slightly nicer to reorder our currents differently from Proposition
3.7
:

Proposition A.2
.

For
p\in I
, we have

\displaystyle\overset{\curvearrowright}{\prod_{a=1}^{n}}\,\overset{\curvearrowleft}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}}E_{p+i}(z_{p+i,a})

\displaystyle=\left((-1)^{\frac{(r-2)(r-3)}{2}+r}\mathfrak{d}^{-\frac{r}{2}+1}\prod_{i\in I}c_{i}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\prod_{i\in I}\frac{\displaystyle\left(1-z_{i,b}/z_{i,a}\right)\left(1-q^{-1}t^{-1}z_{i,b}/z_{i,a}\right)}{\displaystyle\left(1-t^{-1}z_{i+1,b}/z_{i,a}\right)\left(1-q^{-1}z_{i-1,b}/z_{i,a}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\frac{z_{p+1,a}/z_{p,a}}{\left(1-t^{-1}z_{p+1,a}/z_{p,a}\right)\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{p\}}\left(1-q^{-1}z_{i,a}/z_{i+1,a}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in I}\exp\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}\sum_{k>0}\left(p_{k}[X^{(i)}]-t^{-k}p_{k}[X^{(i-1)}]\right)\frac{z_{i,a}^{k}}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in I}\exp\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}\sum_{k>0}\left(-p_{k}[X^{(i)}]^{\perp}+q^{-k}p_{k}[X^{(i-1)}]^{\perp}\right)\frac{z_{i,a}^{-k}}{k}\right)\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{H_{i,0}}

where all rational functions are Laurent series expanded assuming

(A.2)

|z_{i,a}|=1,\,|q|>1,\,|t|>1.

For the
F
-currents, we have

\displaystyle\overset{\curvearrowleft}{\prod_{a=1}^{n}}\,\overset{\curvearrowright}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}}F_{p+i}(z_{p+i,a})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(-1)^{\frac{(r-2)(r-3)}{2}+r}\mathfrak{d}^{\frac{r}{2}-1}}{\displaystyle\prod_{i\in I}c_{i}}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\prod_{i\in I}\frac{\left(1-z_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}{\left(1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\frac{z_{p,a}/z_{p+1,a}}{\left(1-tz_{p,a}/z_{p+1,a}\right)\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{p+1\}}\left(1-qz_{i,a}/z_{i-1,a}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in I}\exp\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}\sum_{k>0}\left(-t^{k}p_{k}[X^{(i)}]+p_{k}[X^{(i-1)}]\right)\frac{z_{i,a}^{k}}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in I}\exp\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}\sum_{k>0}\left(q^{k}p_{k}[X^{(i)}]^{\perp}-p_{k}[X^{(i-1)}]^{\perp}\right)\frac{z_{i,a}^{-k}}{k}\right)\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-H_{i,0}}

where all rational functions are Laurent series expanded assuming

(A.3)

|z_{i,a}|=1,\,|q|<1,\,|t|<1.

A.4.
Integral formula

Let

\displaystyle d_{n}^{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{n(r-1)}t^{-n}\left(1-q^{-1}t^{-1}\right)^{nr}}{v^{-n}\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{-r}t^{-r}\right)},

\displaystyle d_{n}^{-}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{nr}\mathfrak{d}^{-n(r-1)}t^{n}\left(1-qt\right)^{nr}}{v^{n}q^{n}\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{-r}t^{-r}\right)}.

We have

\displaystyle d_{n}^{+}\pi_{N_{\bullet}}\biggl(\left(\rho_{\vec{c}}\circ\Psi_{+}\right)(\mathcal{H}_{p,n}^{-})(f\otimes e^{\alpha})\biggr)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{n}\left(1-q^{-1}t^{-1}\right)^{nr}}{\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{-r}t^{-r}\right)}\left\{\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{a=1}^{n}\prod_{l=1}^{N_{i}}\left(\frac{z_{i+1,a}^{-1}-t^{-1}x_{l}^{(i)}}{z_{i,a}^{-1}-x_{l}^{(i)}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\frac{\left(1-z_{p,b}/z_{p,a}\right)\left(1-q^{-1}t^{-1}z_{p,b}/z_{p,a}\right)}{\left(1-qz_{p+1,b}/z_{p,a}\right)\left(1-q^{-1}z_{p-1,b}/z_{p,a}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\prod_{i\in I\backslash\{p\}}\frac{\left(1-z_{i,b}/z_{i,a}\right)\left(1-q^{-1}t^{-1}z_{i,b}/z_{i,a}\right)}{\left(1-q^{-1}z_{i-1,b}/z_{i,a}\right)\left(1-t^{-1}z_{i+1,b}/z_{i,a}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\left(\frac{z_{0,a}}{z_{p+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-q^{-1}z_{p,a}/z_{p+1,a}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{p\}}\left(\frac{1}{1-q^{-1}z_{i,a}/z_{i+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-t^{-1}z_{i+1,a}/z_{i,a}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in I}f_{i}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{N_{i}}x_{l}^{(i)}-\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-1}+q^{-1}\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i+1,a}^{-1}\right]\Bigg\}_{0}\otimes e^{\alpha}

and

\displaystyle d_{n}^{-}\pi_{N_{\bullet}}\biggl(\left(\rho_{\vec{c}}\circ\Psi_{-}\right)(\mathcal{H}_{p,n}^{+})(f\otimes e^{\alpha})\biggr)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{n}\left(1-qt\right)^{nr}}{\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{r}t^{r}\right)}\left\{\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{a=1}^{n}\prod_{l=1}^{N_{i}}\left(\frac{z_{i,a}^{-1}-tx_{l}^{(i)}}{z_{i+1,a}^{-1}-x_{l}^{(i)}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\frac{\left(1-z_{p+1,a}/z_{p+1,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{p+1,a}/z_{p+1,b}\right)}{\left(1-q^{-1}z_{p,a}/z_{p+1,b}\right)\left(1-qz_{p-2,a}/z_{p+1,b}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\prod_{i\in I\backslash\{p+1\}}\frac{\left(1-z_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}{\left(1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\left(\frac{z_{p+1,a}}{z_{0,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{p+1,a}/z_{p,a}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{p+1\}}\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{i,a}/z_{i-1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,a}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\times\prod_{i\in I}f_{i}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{N_{i}}x_{l}^{(i)}+\sum_{a=1}^{n}qz_{i,a}^{-1}-\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i+1,a}^{-1}\right]\right\}_{0}\otimes e^{\alpha}.

Finally, we make the substitution
w_{i,a}=z_{i,a}^{-1}
and rewrite these formulas in terms of integrals.
This gets us

\displaystyle d_{n}^{+}\pi_{N_{\bullet}}\biggl(\left(\rho_{\vec{c}}\circ\Psi_{+}\right)(\mathcal{H}_{p,n}^{-})(f\otimes e^{\alpha})\biggr)

\displaystyle=\underset{|w_{i,a}|=1}{\oint\cdots\oint}\bigg(\varrho_{p,n}^{+}(w_{\bullet,\bullet},X_{N_{\bullet}})\prod_{i\in I}f_{i}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{N_{i}}x_{l}^{(i)}-\sum_{a=1}^{n}w_{i,a}+\sum_{a=1}^{n}q^{-1}w_{i+1,a}\right]\prod_{a=1}^{n}\frac{dw_{i,a}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}w_{i,a}}\bigg)\otimes e^{\alpha}

and

\displaystyle d_{n}^{-}\pi_{N_{\bullet}}\biggl(\left(\rho_{\vec{c}}\circ\Psi_{-}\right)(\mathcal{H}_{p,n}^{+})(f\otimes e^{\alpha})\biggr)

\displaystyle=\underset{|w_{i,a}|=1}{\oint\cdots\oint}\bigg(\varrho_{p,n}^{-}(w_{\bullet,\bullet},X_{N_{\bullet}})\prod_{i\in I}f_{i}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{N_{i}}x_{l}^{(i)}+\sum_{a=1}^{n}qw_{i,a}-\sum_{a=1}^{n}w_{i+1,a}\right]\prod_{a=1}^{n}\frac{dw_{i,a}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}w_{i,a}}\bigg)\otimes e^{\alpha}

where

\displaystyle\varrho_{p,n}^{+}(w_{\bullet,\bullet},X_{N_{\bullet}})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\left(1-q^{-1}t^{-1}\right)^{nr}}{q^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{-r}t^{-r}\right)}\left\{\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{a=1}^{n}\prod_{l=1}^{N_{i}}\left(\frac{w_{i+1,a}-t^{-1}x_{l}^{(i)}}{w_{i,a}-x_{l}^{(i)}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\frac{\left(w_{p,b}-w_{p,a}\right)\left(w_{p,b}-q^{-1}t^{-1}w_{p,a}\right)}{\left(w_{p,a}-q^{-1}w_{p+1,b}\right)\left(w_{p-1,b}-q^{-1}w_{p,a}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\prod_{i\in I\backslash\{p\}}\frac{\left(w_{i,b}-w_{i,a}\right)\left(w_{i,b}-q^{-1}t^{-1}w_{i,a}\right)}{\left(w_{i-1,b}-q^{-1}w_{i,a}\right)\left(w_{i+1,b}-t^{-1}w_{i,a}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\left(\frac{w_{p+1,a}}{w_{0,a}}\right)\left(\frac{w_{p,a}}{w_{p,a}-q^{-1}w_{p+1,a}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{p\}}\left(\frac{w_{i,a}}{w_{i,a}-q^{-1}w_{i+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{w_{i+1,a}}{w_{i+1,a}-t^{-1}w_{i,a}}\right)\right]

and

\displaystyle\varrho_{p,n}^{-}(w_{\bullet,\bullet},X_{N_{\bullet}})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}q^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\left(1-qt\right)^{nr}}{\prod_{r=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{r}t^{r}\right)}\left\{\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{a=1}^{n}\prod_{l=1}^{N_{i-1}}\left(\frac{w_{i-1,a}-tx_{l}^{(i-1)}}{w_{i,a}-x_{l}^{(i-1)}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\frac{\left(w_{p+1,a}-w_{p+1,b}\right)\left(w_{p+1,a}-qtw_{p+1,b}\right)}{\left(w_{p+1,b}-qw_{p,a}\right)\left(w_{p+2,a}-qw_{p+1,b}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\prod_{i\in I\backslash\{p+1\}}\frac{\left(w_{i,a}-w_{i,b}\right)\left(w_{i,a}-qtw_{i,b}\right)}{\left(w_{i+1,a}-qw_{i,b}\right)\left(w_{i-1,a}-tw_{i,b}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\left(\frac{w_{0,a}}{w_{p+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{w_{p+1,a}}{w_{p+1,a}-qw_{p,a}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\prod_{i\in I\setminus\{p+1\}}\left(\frac{w_{i,a}}{w_{i,a}-qw_{i-1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{w_{i-1,a}}{w_{i-1,a}-tw_{i,a}}\right)\right].

A.5.
Degree one

We compute the integral and record the resulting action on
f
when
n=1
.

A.5.1.
Difference operators

Let
Sh(X_{N_{\bullet}})=Sh
denote the set of
all
shift patterns. Define

\displaystyle H_{p,1}^{*}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1})

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\underline{J}}\in Sh\\
{\underline{J}}\not=\varnothing\end{subarray}}(1-q^{-1}t^{-1})^{|J|-\delta_{p\in J}}\frac{x^{(p+1)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\vartriangle}}}{x^{(0)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\vartriangle}}}\left(\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
x^{(i)}_{l}\not=x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\vartriangle}}\end{subarray}}^{N_{i}}\frac{\left(x_{l}^{(i)}-tx^{(i+1)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\vartriangle}}\right)}{\left(x_{l}^{(i)}-x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\vartriangle}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{qtT_{{\underline{J}}}^{-1}(x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}})-x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}}}{x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}}-T_{{\underline{J}}}^{-1}(x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}})}\right)^{\delta_{p\in J}}\left(\prod_{i\in J\backslash\{p\}}\frac{qtT_{{\underline{J}}}^{-1}(x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}})}{\left(x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}}-T_{\underline{J}}^{-1}(x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}})\right)}\right)T_{{\underline{J}}}^{-1}

\displaystyle H_{p,1}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t^{-1})

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\underline{J}}\in Sh\\
{\underline{J}}\not=\varnothing\end{subarray}}(1-qt)^{|J|-\delta_{p\in J}}\frac{x^{(r-1)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\triangledown}}}{x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\triangledown}}}\left(\prod_{i\in I}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
x^{(i)}_{l}\not=x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\triangledown}}\end{subarray}}^{N_{i}}\frac{\left(x^{(i)}_{l}-t^{-1}x^{(i-1)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\triangledown}}\right)}{\left(x^{(i)}_{l}-x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}^{\triangledown}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{q^{-1}t^{-1}T_{{\underline{J}}}(x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}})-x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}}}{x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}}-T_{{\underline{J}}}(x^{(p)}_{{\underline{J}}})}\right)^{\delta_{p\in J}}\left(\prod_{i\in J\backslash\{p\}}\frac{q^{-1}t^{-1}T_{{\underline{J}}}(x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}})}{\left(x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}}-T_{\underline{J}}(x^{(i)}_{{\underline{J}}})\right)}\right)T_{{\underline{J}}}.

Setting
r=1
and inverting
t
, we indeed obtain the first Noumi-Sano operator.

A.5.2.
Eigenvalues

For a series
f(z)
in
z
, let
[z^{n}]f(z)
denote the coefficient of
z^{n}
.
Methods similar to those in
5.4
allow us to establish the following.

Theorem A.3
.

For
|q|>1
, we have

(A.4)

\displaystyle H^{*}_{p,1}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]

(A.5)

\displaystyle=[z]\left(\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\displaystyle\prod^{|N_{\bullet}|}_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+i+1\end{subarray}}\left(q^{-(\lambda_{b}+i)}t^{-|N_{\bullet}|+(b-1)}z;q^{-r}\right)_{\infty}}{\displaystyle\prod^{|N_{\bullet}|}_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+i\end{subarray}}\left(q^{-(\lambda_{b}+i)}t^{-|N_{\bullet}|+b}z;q^{-r}\right)_{\infty}}\right)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t].

On the other hand, for
|q|<1
, we have

(A.6)

\displaystyle H_{p,1}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]

(A.7)

\displaystyle=[z^{-1}]\left(\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\displaystyle\prod^{|N_{\bullet}|}_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+i+1\end{subarray}}\left(q^{\lambda_{b}+i}t^{|N_{\bullet}|-(b-1)}z^{-1};q^{r}\right)_{\infty}}{\displaystyle\prod^{|N_{\bullet}|}_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
b-\lambda_{b}\equiv p+i\end{subarray}}\left(q^{\lambda_{b}+i}t^{|N_{\bullet}|-b}z^{-1};q^{r}\right)_{\infty}}\right)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t].

Remark A.4
.

We have presented the eigenvalues in terms of our original spectral variables
q^{\lambda_{b}}t^{|N_{\bullet}|-b}
.
However, we can give a more natural combinatorial expression for the eigenvalues if we forgo this and use instead the
transpose
partition
\lambda^{\prime}

[
9
, (I.1.3)]
.
Let

(A.8)

\displaystyle f_{\lambda}(q,t)=\dfrac{1}{1-q}-\sum_{j\geq 1}q^{j-1}t^{|N_{\bullet}|-\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}.

It can be viewed as a series or as a rational function since

(1-q^{r})f_{\lambda}(q,t)
is a polynomial. Let
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}
be the cyclic group and let
\chi
be the generator of
R(\Gamma)
. Define
f_{\lambda}^{(p)}(q,t)
by the following expression in

\mathbb{Q}(q,t)\otimes R(\Gamma)
.

(A.9)

\displaystyle f_{\lambda}(q\chi^{-1},t\chi^{-1})=\chi^{-1}\sum_{p\in I}f^{(p)}_{\lambda}(q,t)\chi^{p}.

Then the eigenvalues are given by

(A.10)

\displaystyle H^{*}_{p,1}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]

\displaystyle=f_{\lambda}^{(p)}(q^{-1},t^{-1})P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]

(A.11)

\displaystyle H_{p,1}(X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t]

\displaystyle=f_{\lambda}^{(p)}(q,t)P_{\lambda}[X_{N_{\bullet}};q,t].

Example A.5
.

Let
r=2
and
\alpha=0
(empty core). We use
N_{0}=N_{1}=1
.
There are three nonempty shift patterns:
{\underline{J}}_{1}=\{x_{1}^{(0)}\}
,
{\underline{J}}_{2}=\{x_{1}^{(1)}\}
, and

{\underline{J}}_{3}=\{x_{1}^{(0)},x_{1}^{(1)}\}
. We apply
H_{0,1}[X_{{N_{\bullet}}};q,t^{-1}]
to
P_{\emptyset}[X_{{N_{\bullet}}};q,t]=1
using summands
{\underline{J}}_{1},{\underline{J}}_{2},{\underline{J}}_{3}
:

\displaystyle-H_{0,1}(X_{{N_{\bullet}}};q,t^{-1})\cdot P_{\emptyset}[X_{{N_{\bullet}}};q,t^{-1}]

\displaystyle=(1-qt)^{0}\dfrac{qx_{1}^{(0)}}{x_{1}^{(0)}}\dfrac{x_{1}^{(1)}-t^{-1}x_{1}^{(0)}}{x_{1}^{(1)}-qx_{1}^{(0)}}\dfrac{q^{-1}t^{-1}q^{2}x_{1}^{(0)}-x_{1}^{(0)}}{x_{1}^{(0)}-q^{2}x_{1}^{(0)}}

\displaystyle+(1-qt)^{1}\dfrac{x_{1}^{(1)}}{qx_{1}^{(1)}}\dfrac{x_{1}^{(0)}-t^{-1}x_{1}^{(1)}}{x_{1}^{(0)}-qx_{1}^{(1)}}\dfrac{q^{-1}t^{-1}q^{2}x_{1}^{(1)}}{x_{1}^{(1)}-q^{2}x_{1}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle+(1-qt)^{1}\dfrac{q^{-1}t^{-1}q^{2}x_{1}^{(0)}-x_{1}^{(0)}}{x_{1}^{(0)}-q^{2}x_{1}^{(0)}}\dfrac{q^{-1}t^{-1}q^{2}x_{1}^{(1)}}{x_{1}^{(1)}-q^{2}x_{1}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{q(qt^{-1}-1)}{1-q^{2}}\dfrac{x_{1}^{(1)}-t^{-1}x_{1}^{(0)}}{x_{1}^{(1)}-qx_{1}^{(0)}}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{(1-qt)t^{-1}}{1-q^{2}}\dfrac{x_{1}^{(0)}-t^{-1}x_{1}^{(1)}}{x_{1}^{(0)}-qx_{1}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{(1-qt)(qt^{-1}-1)qt^{-1}}{(1-q^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{q}{t^{2}}\dfrac{1-t^{2}}{1-q^{2}}P_{\emptyset}[X_{{N_{\bullet}}};q,t^{-1}].

We have

\displaystyle f_{\emptyset}(q,t)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{1-q}-\dfrac{t^{2}}{1-q}=\dfrac{1-t^{2}}{1-q}

\displaystyle f_{\emptyset}(q\chi^{-1},t\chi^{-1})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1-t^{2}}{1-q^{2}}(1+q\chi)

\displaystyle f_{\emptyset}^{(0)}(q,t)

\displaystyle=q\dfrac{1-t^{2}}{1-q^{2}}

\displaystyle f_{\emptyset}^{(0)}(q,t^{-1})

\displaystyle=q\dfrac{1-t^{-2}}{1-q^{2}}=-\dfrac{q}{t^{2}}\dfrac{1-t^{2}}{1-q^{2}}.
